order=FBL-020-R6 §1.1 acceptance=NOT_REVIEWED
role=OPERATOR_CONTROLLED_HOST may_decide_the_057_058_branch=true
This census inspected an operator-controlled host: a machine that keeps state across restarts and is not created and destroyed by a job. Its scope is that one host and nothing beyond it - see census_scope.out_of_scope. It states a position and the evidence for it; that is the implementer's reading offered for review, and `acceptance` is NOT_REVIEWED.

head=174c7893c8fd05d1fabf0d8ad97eafa168c35fc6 tree_state=modified modified_paths=51 migrations_match_head=false
Taken from a working tree carrying 51 changed path(s) on top of the commit above; migrations/ carries 1 of them. The markers this census searched for are derived from migrations/, so that number is the one that decides whether this census asked the same question the commit would have asked.

scope=the GitHub Actions workflows declared in .github/workflows, read as source | registered Windows services whose image is a PostgreSQL server | every PostgreSQL data directory discoverable on this host | the named volume backing docker-compose.yml's postgres service | the PostgreSQL clusters inside each WSL distribution | the local drill cluster on 127.0.0.1:55434
out-of-scope: Any environment not reachable from this host. A managed database, a colleague machine, a customer deployment and a cloud environment nobody on this machine has credentials for are all outside this census, and nothing here is evidence about them.
out-of-scope: Any host on the network. Only 127.0.0.1 is probed, and only for the local drill port.
out-of-scope: Any cluster whose data directory this account cannot read. Such a cluster is reported `indeterminate`, never `no`.
out-of-scope: The future. Every verdict is a reading at `generated_at`; an environment migrated a minute later is not described here.

This document REPORTS a finding and states the implementer’s reading of it. It is NOT an acceptance, ratification or discharge by the architect or by anyone else, and it must not be cited as one.

THE ENVIRONMENT COUNT IN THIS ARTIFACT IS A POINT-IN-TIME READING OF ONE HOST, NOT A DURABLE FIGURE. This machine creates and destroys scratch PostgreSQL clusters under the OS temporary directory as a matter of course, including for work unrelated to this order, so the number of directories the sweep enumerates changes between runs and even during a single run. The durable claim of this census is `conclusion.position` — whether any PERSISTENT environment holds a form of migration 057 — and that is what §0.2 turns on and what the delivery documents quote. Do not read the count as a measurement of anything.

markers=057_identity_boundary_completion.sql, 058_policy_evidence_reconstructable.sql

environment=github-actions-ci
  persistence=ephemeral inspected=true
  migration_057_applied=no
  method=indentation-aware read of every workflow file in .github/workflows: the `services:` blocks, any `volumes:` key inside one, any bind mount smuggled through `options:`, and any step that deploys to a long-lived environment
    workflow files present: ci.yml
    triggers: ci.yml: push|pull_request
    jobs declaring service containers: ci.yml:verify → postgres ; ci.yml:migration-upgrade → postgres
    service images: verify/postgres postgres:16-alpine@sha256:57c72fd2a128e416c7fcc499958864df5301e940bca0a56f58fddf30ffc07777 ; migration-upgrade/postgres postgres:16-alpine@sha256:57c72fd2a128e416c7fcc499958864df5301e940bca0a56f58fddf30ffc07777
    volumes: keys inside a service definition: none
    bind mounts inside a service options: string: none
    steps that deploy to a long-lived environment: none
  basis=every database in CI lives in a service container with no volume and no bind mount, so its filesystem is destroyed with the job; and no workflow deploys to anywhere that would keep a schema. Each run migrates a database that did not exist before it.
  limit: this is a claim about the workflows AS COMMITTED. A workflow_dispatch run of a different revision, or a job on a self-hosted runner with pre-existing state, is outside what this file can see
  limit: it says nothing about GitHub-side caches; the actions/setup-node cache holds npm packages, not a database directory
